This means any piece of mine that goes to f4 or f6 will be taken by the bishop.
Any piece that goes to e3 will be taken by the king.

The bishop at e3 cannot attack the rook at f3.
The king at d3 cannot attack the rook at f3.
So the Rook at f3 is perfectly safe!

g3 is a dark square. e3 is a dark square.
We already checked that they are not on the same diagonal.

g3 is not on any of those.
So the bishop at g3 is also safe.

White's Knight is at c6. It's a light-squared knight.
The Rook at f3 is on a light square.
Rooks attack in straight lines.

If I move the Rook to f6, it attacks c6.
But as we saw, `f3f6` allows `Be3xf6`.

f5 is a light square (f=6, 5=5. 6+5=11=light).

If the Rook is at c5, it attacks the Knight at c6!

What if I move the bishop? `g3d6`.
d6 is a dark square. The Bishop at e3 cannot attack it. (Diagonal of e3: f4, e5, d6... wait! e3, d4, c5... no. Let's re-check e3 diagonal: e3-d4-c5-b6-a7, e3-f4-g5-h6, e3-d2-c1, e3-f2-g1).

`f3f5` starts a plan to attack the knight.
